Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Is it the sight of the bridge itself that triggers your anxiety, or is it something else about the experience of being on the bridge?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's definitely just the sight of it. Like, as soon as I see the bridge coming up in the distance, my chest gets all tight, and my hands start sweating on the steering wheel. I just end up gripping it so hard my knuckles turn white, and I have to stare straight ahead, like I just have to make it to the other side as fast as possible. It's the same feeling I got when I had to give that presentation at work last year. We were rolling out this new benefits package, and my boss made me stand up in front of the whole department. I hate public speaking. My mouth gets dry as a bone. I actually kept a bottle of water up there with me, one of those fancy Fiji waters in the square bottle. My wife buys them by the case at Costco. You know, Costco is great, but you end up buying way more than you need. We went in for paper towels once and came out with a gazebo. A whole gazebo! Took me three weekends to put it together in the backyard. The instructions were terrible, just pictures, no words. \n\nBut, uh... where was I? Oh, the bridges. Yeah, it's just seeing them. I don't even have to be on it yet. Just knowing I have to cross it and seeing it there... that's what gets me going.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety triggered by the height of the bridge or by the fact that you have to cross over water or a deep gap?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... I think it's the height, mostly. And the water. I know those bridges over the bay are built fine, you know? Like, folks drive them every day without a second thought. But when I get near one, my mind just acts like the car could go right over the side. It's crazy. Speaking of cars, I actually just had to take my Honda in for an oil change last week. The guy at the shop, Jerry, he's been working on my cars for years. He tried to tell me I needed new brake pads, but I just had them done like, I don't know, maybe two years ago? I told him to just do the oil. They always try to upsell you on something, you know? Like the air filters. I can change an air filter myself in five minutes, I'm not paying them forty bucks to do it. Jerry's a good guy, though. He coaches Little League with my brother-in-law. They had a really good season last year, made it all the way to the regionals. My nephew plays first base. Kid's got a swing like you wouldn't believe, but he strikes out a lot because he always swings for the fences. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the gap. Yeah, it's just... looking over the edge, seeing how far down it is to the water. It just makes me feel like we're gonna fall right off.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety triggered more by the fact that the bridge is over water, or would you feel just as anxious crossing a high bridge over land?\n\nPatient: Well, you know, I... I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about it like that. I don't think I cross a lot of high bridges over land. Most of the ones around here go over the water, like the big one heading out towards the shore. We used to go to the shore every summer when the kids were little. Rented a house down in Cape May. Beautiful Victorian houses down there. My wife loved the porches, always wanted to put a wrap-around porch on our house, but the zoning board wouldn't let us because of how close we are to the property line. The guy next door, Mr. Henderson, he's a real stickler for the rules. He actually called the town on us once because our grass was, like, half an inch over the limit. Can you believe that? Half an inch! I had to go out there on a Sunday morning and mow it just to get him to back off. My mower is getting pretty old, too. It's a Toro, had it for maybe twelve years. Still starts on the first pull, though, if you prime it right. You gotta push that little red bulb exactly three times, no more, no less. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... over land or over water? I guess I just don't really know for sure since I try to avoid them all anyway.\n\n[Last Question]\nWould you feel anxious crossing a very high bridge that goes over solid land?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 54 / human_resources_worker / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, like I said, I... I don't really know if I'd feel any different if it was over land. I just don't run into those very often. There is that one overpass on the interstate, I guess, the one where they've been doing construction for the last six months. It's a nightmare trying to get through there during rush hour. Traffic just backs up for miles. I usually try to take the back roads, but then you get stuck behind the school buses. My daughter used to ride the bus, but she's driving now. Just got her license last month. She's a pretty good driver, actually, a lot better than my son was at her age. He backed right into the mailbox his first week having his license. Completely snapped the wooden post in half. I had to go to Home Depot and buy a whole new setup, get the quick-setting concrete, the whole nine yards. Digging that hole was a pain, too. Hit a huge rock about a foot down and had to dig around it. Took me all Saturday morning. 

But, uh... a high bridge over land? I honestly couldn't tell you for sure. I just try to stay away from anything high up if I can help it.

Tall buildings don't bother me at all. Actually, our old HR office used to be on the 14th floor of this big high-rise downtown before we moved out to the business park. I told him he should have just put the spare on himself, but he said the jack was rusted out. You really gotta check your tools before winter hits, you know? We actually went to the eye doctor last week, Dr. Henderson over in the strip mall next to the Target? Nice guy, but his waiting room is freezing. I don't know why doctors' offices always keep the AC cranked up so high. You sit there for twenty minutes and you're shivering by the time they call your name. Anyway, he told her she needs bifocals now, which she wasn't too happy about. My buddy Carl has them and he swears by them. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... what was the question? Oh, being a passenger. Yeah, I don't know, honestly. I'm almost always the one behind the wheel, so I couldn't really tell you if it's just as bad when I'm riding shotgun. I just know when I'm driving, it's... it's a whole ordeal.\n\nDoctor: If you were a passenger in a car going over a high bridge, would you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I'd still be pretty worked up even if I wasn't the one driving. I mean, just the sight of those bridges gets to me. As soon as I see the bridge coming up in the distance, my chest gets all tight, and my hands start sweating. Even if I was in the passenger seat, I think I'd still be gripping the door handle or something, just staring straight ahead, feeling like we just have to make it to the other side, you know? \n\nIt's funny, speaking of the passenger seat, my wife has this habit of leaving her empty coffee cups in the door pocket on her side. I swear, every time I go to vacuum out the car—which I try to do every other weekend, especially with the dog tracking in dirt—I find like three of those paper cups from the Dunkin' down the street. I keep telling her, \"Honey, just throw them out when we get gas,\" but she always forgets. We've got this great little hand vac, though, a Dirt Devil. Got it on sale at Home Depot last Black Friday. Best thirty bucks I ever spent. It gets right into the crevices of the seats. \n\nBut, uh, wait... oh, right, being a passenger on the bridge. Yeah, I'm pretty sure my chest would still get tight and I'd be sweating just as bad, even if I wasn't the one steering. Just seeing the bridge is enough to set it off.\n\nDoctor: Has avoiding bridges or being late to work due to taking the long way around them affected your relationships with your coworkers or your overall job performance?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it hasn't really messed up my job or anything. I still get to work every day and do my job in HR, and I see people just fine. I just have to leave extra early to take the long way around the bridges. It's not a huge deal, but I do have to wake up at like 5:30 AM now. Which is fine, I guess, because I like to catch the morning news anyway. You know, they have that new anchor on Channel 4, the guy with the really great hair? He used to do the weather in Chicago, I think. My wife thinks he's too loud, but I like him. Water everywhere, right in the front yard. Had to call a plumber on a Sunday, which you know costs an arm and a leg. He charged me double just for showing up. \n\nBut, uh, what was I saying? Oh, right, work and stuff. Yeah, my job's fine. It's more just... I don't know, it makes me feel less independent than I used to be. Like, I've had to turn down a few fishing outings with the guys because the lake is over the big suspension bridge. I just told them I was busy, but really I just didn't want to deal with the drive. You know, they let you check two bags for free, which is great because my wife always overpacks. She brought like four pairs of shoes for a four-day trip. We went to this amazing seafood place right on the water down there. Best grouper sandwich I ever had. They served it with these homemade kettle chips that were just perfectly crispy. The waiter was a young kid, maybe 19, told us he was studying marine biology at the local college. Smart kid, very polite. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what were you asking? Oh, control. Yeah, no, I don't get anxious about that kind of stuff. It's literally just the high bridges. I don't know what it is about them, but nothing else really bothers me like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever walked or biked across a high bridge, and if so, did you experience anxiety in that situation?\n\nPatient: Walk or bike? Oh, no, I haven't tried that. I haven't ridden a bike in... gosh, it's gotta be fifteen years. I used to have this great mountain bike, a Schwinn, back when we lived in our old neighborhood. Never won more than five bucks, though. My neighbor, Tom, he actually won like fifty grand on a scratch-off once. Can you believe that? He used it to put a sunroom on the back of his house. It was really nice, all glass, but it got so hot in the summer you couldn't even sit out there. He had to buy this massive ceiling fan just to keep the air moving. I helped him install it, actually. We almost dropped the motor right on his glass coffee table. \n\nBut, uh, what was the question? Oh, walking or biking over a bridge. Yeah, no, I haven't done that. I can't even imagine it. Best thirty bucks I ever spent. It gets right into the crevices of the seats. \n\nBut, uh, wait... oh, right, being a passenger on the bridge. Yeah, I'm pretty sure my chest would still get tight and I'd be sweating just as bad, even if I wasn't the one steering. Just seeing the bridge is enough to set it off.\n\nDoctor: Has avoiding bridges or being late to work due to taking the long way around them affected your relationships with your coworkers or your overall job performance?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it hasn't really messed up my job or anything. I still get to work every day and do my job in HR, and I see people just fine. I just have to leave extra early to take the long way around the bridges. It's not a huge deal, but I do have to wake up at like 5:30 AM now. Which is fine, I guess, because I like to catch the morning news anyway. You know, they have that new anchor on Channel 4, the guy with the really great hair? He used to do the weather in Chicago, I think. My wife thinks he's too loud, but I like him. He actually did a whole segment on how to properly winterize your lawn sprinkler system last week. I meant to write down the steps, because last year one of my pipes burst and it was a nightmare. Water everywhere, right in the front yard. Had to call a plumber on a Sunday, which you know costs an arm and a leg. He charged me double just for showing up. \n\nBut, uh, what was I saying? Oh, right, work and stuff. Yeah, my job's fine. It's more just... I don't know, it makes me feel less independent than I used to be. Like, I've had to turn down a few fishing outings with the guys because the lake is over the big suspension bridge. I just told them I was busy, but really I just didn't want to deal with the drive. It's a shame, too, because they caught a ton of bass last time. My buddy Steve has this new fiberglass boat, a real beauty. He got it at a boat show in February. I think he paid too much for it, honestly, but he loves it. Anyway, yeah, I still get by, it's just... annoying having to plan around it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience similar anxiety in other situations where you feel like you're not in control?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think it's about being in control, because like I said, even if I'm not driving, just seeing the bridge gets me all worked up. It's really just the bridges. I fly on planes just fine, you know? Actually, my wife and I flew down to Florida last winter to visit her sister in Tampa. We flew Southwest. You know, they let you check two bags for free, which is great because my wife always overpacks. She brought like four pairs of shoes for a four-day trip. We went to this amazing seafood place right on the water down there. Best grouper sandwich I ever had. They served it with these homemade kettle chips that were just perfectly crispy. The waiter was a young kid, maybe 19, told us he was studying marine biology at the local college. Smart kid, very polite. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what were you asking? Oh, control. Yeah, no, I don't get anxious about that kind of stuff. It's literally just the high bridges. I don't know what it is about them, but nothing else really bothers me like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever walked or biked across a high bridge, and if so, did you experience anxiety in that situation?\n\nPatient: Walk or bike? Oh, no, I haven't tried that. I haven't ridden a bike in... gosh, it's gotta be fifteen years. I used to have this great mountain bike, a Schwinn, back when we lived in our old neighborhood. It had twenty-one gears and these really thick tires. I used to ride it down to the corner store on Saturday mornings to get the paper and a couple of scratch-offs. Never won more than five bucks, though. My neighbor, Tom, he actually won like fifty grand on a scratch-off once. Can you believe that? He used it to put a sunroom on the back of his house. It was really nice, all glass, but it got so hot in the summer you couldn't even sit out there. He had to buy this massive ceiling fan just to keep the air moving. I helped him install it, actually. We almost dropped the motor right on his glass coffee table. \n\nBut, uh, what was the question? Oh, walking or biking over a bridge. Yeah, no, I haven't done that. I can't even imagine it. If just driving over one or seeing it from the passenger seat gets me sweating and my chest all tight, I think walking across one would probably be a hundred times worse. Just being out there in the open air, looking down... no thanks. I wouldn't even try it.\n\nDoctor: Is it the sight of the bridge itself that triggers your anxiety, or is it something else about the experience of being on the bridge?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's definitely just the sight of it. Like, as soon as I see the bridge coming up in the distance, my chest gets all tight, and my hands start sweating on the steering wheel. I just end up gripping it so hard my knuckles turn white, and I have to stare straight ahead, like I just have to make it to the other side as fast as possible. It's the same feeling I got when I had to give that presentation at work last year. We were rolling out this new benefits package, and my boss made me stand up in front of the whole department. I hate public speaking. My mouth gets dry as a bone. I actually kept a bottle of water up there with me, one of those fancy Fiji waters in the square bottle. My wife buys them by the case at Costco. You know, Costco is great, but you end up buying way more than you need. We went in for paper towels once and came out with a gazebo. A whole gazebo! Took me three weekends to put it together in the backyard. The instructions were terrible, just pictures, no words. \n\nBut, uh... where was I? Oh, the bridges. Yeah, it's just seeing them. I don't even have to be on it yet. Just knowing I have to cross it and seeing it there... that's what gets me going.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety triggered by the height of the bridge or by the fact that you have to cross over water or a deep gap?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... I think it's the height, mostly. And the water. I know those bridges over the bay are built fine, you know? Like, folks drive them every day without a second thought. But when I get near one, my mind just acts like the car could go right over the side. It's crazy. Speaking of cars, I actually just had to take my Honda in for an oil change last week. The guy at the shop, Jerry, he's been working on my cars for years. He tried to tell me I needed new brake pads, but I just had them done like, I don't know, maybe two years ago? I told him to just do the oil. They always try to upsell you on something, you know? Like the air filters. I can change an air filter myself in five minutes, I'm not paying them forty bucks to do it. Jerry's a good guy, though. He coaches Little League with my brother-in-law. They had a really good season last year, made it all the way to the regionals. My nephew plays first base. Kid's got a swing like you wouldn't believe, but he strikes out a lot because he always swings for the fences. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the gap. Yeah, it's just... looking over the edge, seeing how far down it is to the water. It just makes me feel like we're gonna fall right off.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety triggered more by the fact that the bridge is over water, or would you feel just as anxious crossing a high bridge over land?\n\nPatient: Well, you know, I... I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about it like that. I don't think I cross a lot of high bridges over land. Most of the ones around here go over the water, like the big one heading out towards the shore. We used to go to the shore every summer when the kids were little. Rented a house down in Cape May. Beautiful Victorian houses down there. My wife loved the porches, always wanted to put a wrap-around porch on our house, but the zoning board wouldn't let us because of how close we are to the property line. The guy next door, Mr. Henderson, he's a real stickler for the rules. He actually called the town on us once because our grass was, like, half an inch over the limit. Can you believe that? Half an inch! I had to go out there on a Sunday morning and mow it just to get him to back off. My mower is getting pretty old, too. It's a Toro, had it for maybe twelve years. Still starts on the first pull, though, if you prime it right. You gotta push that little red bulb exactly three times, no more, no less. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... over land or over water? I guess I just don't really know for sure since I try to avoid them all anyway.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious crossing a very high bridge that goes over solid land?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, I... I don't really know if I'd feel any different if it was over land. I just don't run into those very often. There is that one overpass on the interstate, I guess, the one where they've been doing construction for the last six months. It's a nightmare trying to get through there during rush hour. Traffic just backs up for miles. I usually try to take the back roads, but then you get stuck behind the school buses. My daughter used to ride the bus, but she's driving now. Just got her license last month. She's a pretty good driver, actually, a lot better than my son was at her age. He backed right into the mailbox his first week having his license. Completely snapped the wooden post in half. I had to go to Home Depot and buy a whole new setup, get the quick-setting concrete, the whole nine yards. Digging that hole was a pain, too. Hit a huge rock about a foot down and had to dig around it. Took me all Saturday morning. \n\nBut, uh... a high bridge over land? I honestly couldn't tell you for sure. I just try to stay away from anything high up if I can help it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, a deep valley... I really couldn't say if that'd be any better. I just don't like the height part, you know? Valleys actually remind me of when my brother-in-law went out to Colorado a couple years back. He went hiking somewhere near the Rockies, thought he was in great shape, but the altitude completely knocked him out. He was sick for three days straight in his hotel room. 

Speaking of being out sick, work has been a total headache lately with that kind of stuff. I work in HR over at the distribution center, and we've got this one guy on the floor, Gary, who keeps trying to put in for medical leave every time he wants to take a ski trip. He thinks we don't check his social media, but I saw him posting pictures from Aspen when he was supposedly laid up with a bad back. I had to call him into the office on Tuesday and have a whole sit-down with him and the union rep. The union rep, Linda, she's always bringing these terrible store-bought muffins to our meetings. Like, the really dry ones that just crumble all over the desk and leave grease spots on the paperwork. 

But, uh, wait, what was the question? Oh, right, a bridge over a valley. Yeah, I honestly have no idea if that would be less scary than the water. Like I said, I just hate being up high period, so I wouldn't really want to test it out either way.

    "role": "system",

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF40.231, F40.248\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, what brings me in is... I keep planning my whole day around not having to drive over those high bridges. Like that big suspension one on the interstate? They built that back in, what, '89? My buddy Carl actually worked on the construction crew for it. He used to tell me these crazy stories about the scaffolding and how the wind would just howl up there. Carl's retired now, actually. Moved down to Florida, bought a pontoon boat... I don't know how he deals with a boat, honestly, the maintenance alone is a total nightmare. \n\nBut, uh, anyway, yeah. The bridges. If I absolutely can't find a route around them, I just white-knuckle the steering wheel the whole way across. Just gripping it for dear life. But mostly I take the long way around to avoid them, which makes me late for work sometimes. And working in HR, I really shouldn't be late. We had this one guy last week showing up at 10 AM every single day, just strolling in like he owned the place, and I had to be the one to sit him down and talk about company time... but yeah, the driving thing is just getting to be a real hassle.\n\nDoctor: Do you also avoid other high places, like tall buildings or mountainous areas, or is it mainly just the bridges that bother you?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the bridges. Tall buildings don't bother me at all. Actually, our old HR office used to be on the 14th floor of this big high-rise downtown before we moved out to the business park. The elevators in that place, though... man, they were always breaking down. I swear, at least once a month somebody would get stuck in one. We had this maintenance guy, Hector—great guy, knew everything about HVAC—but he was always complaining about the elevator parts being on backorder. Supply chain issues, you know? \n\nIt's like you can't get anything on time anymore. I ordered a new filter for my home furnace like three weeks ago and it's still just sitting in some warehouse in Ohio, according to the tracking number. And you really need to change those every few months or your heating bill goes right through the roof. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, heights. Yeah, no, mountains and buildings are totally fine. Just those high bridges for some reason.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid that the bridge will collapse while you are driving on it?\n\nPatient: No, no, I don't think they're going to collapse. I mean, I know those bridges over the bay are built fine and folks drive them every single day without a problem. My brother-in-law commutes over one every morning in his Chevy Silverado. Actually, he just got new tires on that thing, those big all-terrain ones. He got a flat last month on his way to work and had to wait three hours for a tow truck. Three hours! I told him he should have just put the spare on himself, but he said the jack was rusted out. You really gotta check your tools before winter hits, you know? The salt on the roads around here just eats right through the metal. I usually take my car to the wash at least twice a month during the winter just to spray the undercarriage. They have this new automated one down by the grocery store that does a really good job with the wax, too, and it's only like eight bucks if you buy the monthly pass. \n\nBut, uh... sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the bridge falling down. Yeah, I'm not worried about the engineering or anything like that. It's just... when I get near one, my mind just acts like the car could go right over the side. Like I'm just going to veer off the edge or something.\n\nDoctor: Does your anxiety occur when you are a passenger in a car driving over a high bridge, or is it only when you are the one driving?\n\nPatient: Well, it's funny you ask that, because I haven't actually been a passenger over one in a long time. My wife usually makes me drive everywhere. She hates driving, especially at night. Her night vision isn't what it used to be. We actually went to the eye doctor last week, Dr. Henderson over in the strip mall next to the Target? Nice guy, but his waiting room is freezing. I don't know why doctors' offices always keep the AC cranked up so high. You sit there for twenty minutes and you're shivering by the time they call your name. Anyway, he told her she needs bifocals now, which she wasn't too happy about. She thinks they make her look old, but I told her, hey, they have those progressive lenses now where you can't even see the line. My buddy Carl has them and he swears by them. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... what was the question? Oh, being a passenger. Yeah, I don't know, honestly. I'm almost always the one behind the wheel, so I couldn't really tell you if it's just as bad when I'm riding shotgun. I just know when I'm driving, it's... it's a whole ordeal.\n\nDoctor: If you were a passenger in a car going over a high bridge, would you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I'd still be pretty worked up even if I wasn't the one driving. I mean, just the sight of those bridges gets to me. As soon as I see the bridge coming up in the distance, my chest gets all tight, and my hands start sweating. Even if I was in the passenger seat, I think I'd still be gripping the door handle or something, just staring straight ahead, feeling like we just have to make it to the other side, you know? \n\nIt's funny, speaking of the passenger seat, my wife has this habit of leaving her empty coffee cups in the door pocket on her side. I swear, every time I go to vacuum out the car—which I try to do every other weekend, especially with the dog tracking in dirt—I find like three of those paper cups from the Dunkin' down the street. I keep telling her, \"Honey, just throw them out when we get gas,\" but she always forgets. We've got this great little hand vac, though, a Dirt Devil. Got it on sale at Home Depot last Black Friday. Best thirty bucks I ever spent. It gets right into the crevices of the seats. \n\nBut, uh, wait... oh, right, being a passenger on the bridge. Yeah, I'm pretty sure my chest would still get tight and I'd be sweating just as bad, even if I wasn't the one steering. Just seeing the bridge is enough to set it off.\n\nDoctor: Has avoiding bridges or being late to work due to taking the long way around them affected your relationships with your coworkers or your overall job performance?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it hasn't really messed up my job or anything. I still get to work every day and do my job in HR, and I see people just fine. I just have to leave extra early to take the long way around the bridges. It's not a huge deal, but I do have to wake up at like 5:30 AM now. Which is fine, I guess, because I like to catch the morning news anyway. You know, they have that new anchor on Channel 4, the guy with the really great hair? He used to do the weather in Chicago, I think. My wife thinks he's too loud, but I like him. He actually did a whole segment on how to properly winterize your lawn sprinkler system last week. I meant to write down the steps, because last year one of my pipes burst and it was a nightmare. Water everywhere, right in the front yard. Had to call a plumber on a Sunday, which you know costs an arm and a leg. He charged me double just for showing up. \n\nBut, uh, what was I saying? Oh, right, work and stuff. Yeah, my job's fine. It's more just... I don't know, it makes me feel less independent than I used to be. Like, I've had to turn down a few fishing outings with the guys because the lake is over the big suspension bridge. I just told them I was busy, but really I just didn't want to deal with the drive. It's a shame, too, because they caught a ton of bass last time. My buddy Steve has this new fiberglass boat, a real beauty. He got it at a boat show in February. I think he paid too much for it, honestly, but he loves it. Anyway, yeah, I still get by, it's just... annoying having to plan around it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience similar anxiety in other situations where you feel like you're not in control?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think it's about being in control, because like I said, even if I'm not driving, just seeing the bridge gets me all worked up. It's really just the bridges. I fly on planes just fine, you know? Actually, my wife and I flew down to Florida last winter to visit her sister in Tampa. We flew Southwest. You know, they let you check two bags for free, which is great because my wife always overpacks. She brought like four pairs of shoes for a four-day trip. We went to this amazing seafood place right on the water down there. Best grouper sandwich I ever had. They served it with these homemade kettle chips that were just perfectly crispy. The waiter was a young kid, maybe 19, told us he was studying marine biology at the local college. Smart kid, very polite. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what were you asking? Oh, control. Yeah, no, I don't get anxious about that kind of stuff. It's literally just the high bridges. I don't know what it is about them, but nothing else really bothers me like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever walked or biked across a high bridge, and if so, did you experience anxiety in that situation?\n\nPatient: Walk or bike? Oh, no, I haven't tried that. I haven't ridden a bike in... gosh, it's gotta be fifteen years. I used to have this great mountain bike, a Schwinn, back when we lived in our old neighborhood. It had twenty-one gears and these really thick tires. I used to ride it down to the corner store on Saturday mornings to get the paper and a couple of scratch-offs. Never won more than five bucks, though. My neighbor, Tom, he actually won like fifty grand on a scratch-off once. Can you believe that? He used it to put a sunroom on the back of his house. It was really nice, all glass, but it got so hot in the summer you couldn't even sit out there. He had to buy this massive ceiling fan just to keep the air moving. I helped him install it, actually. We almost dropped the motor right on his glass coffee table. \n\nBut, uh, what was the question? Oh, walking or biking over a bridge. Yeah, no, I haven't done that. I can't even imagine it. If just driving over one or seeing it from the passenger seat gets me sweating and my chest all tight, I think walking across one would probably be a hundred times worse. Just being out there in the open air, looking down... no thanks. I wouldn't even try it.\n\nDoctor: Is it the sight of the bridge itself that triggers your anxiety, or is it something else about the experience of being on the bridge?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's definitely just the sight of it. Like, as soon as I see the bridge coming up in the distance, my chest gets all tight, and my hands start sweating on the steering wheel. I just end up gripping it so hard my knuckles turn white, and I have to stare straight ahead, like I just have to make it to the other side as fast as possible. It's the same feeling I got when I had to give that presentation at work last year. We were rolling out this new benefits package, and my boss made me stand up in front of the whole department. I hate public speaking. My mouth gets dry as a bone. I actually kept a bottle of water up there with me, one of those fancy Fiji waters in the square bottle. My wife buys them by the case at Costco. You know, Costco is great, but you end up buying way more than you need. We went in for paper towels once and came out with a gazebo. A whole gazebo! Took me three weekends to put it together in the backyard. The instructions were terrible, just pictures, no words. \n\nBut, uh... where was I? Oh, the bridges. Yeah, it's just seeing them. I don't even have to be on it yet. Just knowing I have to cross it and seeing it there... that's what gets me going.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety triggered by the height of the bridge or by the fact that you have to cross over water or a deep gap?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... I think it's the height, mostly. And the water. I know those bridges over the bay are built fine, you know? Like, folks drive them every day without a second thought. But when I get near one, my mind just acts like the car could go right over the side. It's crazy. Speaking of cars, I actually just had to take my Honda in for an oil change last week. The guy at the shop, Jerry, he's been working on my cars for years. He tried to tell me I needed new brake pads, but I just had them done like, I don't know, maybe two years ago? I told him to just do the oil. They always try to upsell you on something, you know? Like the air filters. I can change an air filter myself in five minutes, I'm not paying them forty bucks to do it. Jerry's a good guy, though. He coaches Little League with my brother-in-law. They had a really good season last year, made it all the way to the regionals. My nephew plays first base. Kid's got a swing like you wouldn't believe, but he strikes out a lot because he always swings for the fences. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the gap. Yeah, it's just... looking over the edge, seeing how far down it is to the water. It just makes me feel like we're gonna fall right off.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety triggered more by the fact that the bridge is over water, or would you feel just as anxious crossing a high bridge over land?\n\nPatient: Well, you know, I... I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about it like that. I don't think I cross a lot of high bridges over land. Most of the ones around here go over the water, like the big one heading out towards the shore. We used to go to the shore every summer when the kids were little. Rented a house down in Cape May. Beautiful Victorian houses down there. My wife loved the porches, always wanted to put a wrap-around porch on our house, but the zoning board wouldn't let us because of how close we are to the property line. The guy next door, Mr. Henderson, he's a real stickler for the rules. He actually called the town on us once because our grass was, like, half an inch over the limit. Can you believe that? Half an inch! I had to go out there on a Sunday morning and mow it just to get him to back off. My mower is getting pretty old, too. It's a Toro, had it for maybe twelve years. Still starts on the first pull, though, if you prime it right. You gotta push that little red bulb exactly three times, no more, no less. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... over land or over water? I guess I just don't really know for sure since I try to avoid them all anyway.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious crossing a very high bridge that goes over solid land?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, I... I don't really know if I'd feel any different if it was over land. I just don't run into those very often. There is that one overpass on the interstate, I guess, the one where they've been doing construction for the last six months. It's a nightmare trying to get through there during rush hour. Traffic just backs up for miles. I usually try to take the back roads, but then you get stuck behind the school buses. My daughter used to ride the bus, but she's driving now. Just got her license last month. She's a pretty good driver, actually, a lot better than my son was at her age. He backed right into the mailbox his first week having his license. Completely snapped the wooden post in half. I had to go to Home Depot and buy a whole new setup, get the quick-setting concrete, the whole nine yards. Digging that hole was a pain, too. Hit a huge rock about a foot down and had to dig around it. Took me all Saturday morning. \n\nBut, uh... a high bridge over land? I honestly couldn't tell you for sure. I just try to stay away from anything high up if I can help it.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel less anxious if you had to cross a very high bridge that goes over a deep valley with solid land at the bottom, rather than over water?\n\nPatient: Well, a deep valley... I really couldn't say if that'd be any better. I just don't like the height part, you know? Valleys actually remind me of when my brother-in-law went out to Colorado a couple years back. He went hiking somewhere near the Rockies, thought he was in great shape, but the altitude completely knocked him out. He was sick for three days straight in his hotel room. \n\nSpeaking of being out sick, work has been a total headache lately with that kind of stuff. I work in HR over at the distribution center, and we've got this one guy on the floor, Gary, who keeps trying to put in for medical leave every time he wants to take a ski trip. He thinks we don't check his social media, but I saw him posting pictures from Aspen when he was supposedly laid up with a bad back. I had to call him into the office on Tuesday and have a whole sit-down with him and the union rep. The union rep, Linda, she's always bringing these terrible store-bought muffins to our meetings. Like, the really dry ones that just crumble all over the desk and leave grease spots on the paperwork. \n\nBut, uh, wait, what was the question? Oh, right, a bridge over a valley. Yeah, I honestly have no idea if that would be less scary than the water. Like I said, I just hate being up high period, so I wouldn't really want to test it out either way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Well, an enclosed bridge... I don't know, maybe? I haven't really been on one of those. I mean, if I can't see the water, maybe it wouldn't be as bad. But then again, tunnels can be pretty awful too. I told him to just do the oil. They always try to upsell you on something, you know? Like the air filters. I can change an air filter myself in five minutes, I'm not paying them forty bucks to do it. Kid's got a swing like you wouldn't believe, but he strikes out a lot because he always swings for the fences. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the gap. Yeah, it's just... looking over the edge, seeing how far down it is to the water. Most of the ones around here go over the water, like the big one heading out towards the shore. We used to go to the shore every summer when the kids were little. Rented a house down in Cape May. Beautiful Victorian houses down there. My wife loved the porches, always wanted to put a wrap-around porch on our house, but the zoning board wouldn't let us because of how close we are to the property line. The guy next door, Mr. Henderson, he's a real stickler for the rules. He actually called the town on us once because our grass was, like, half an inch over the limit. Can you believe that? Half an inch! I had to go out there on a Sunday morning and mow it just to get him to back off. My mower is getting pretty old, too. It's a Toro, had it for maybe twelve years. Still starts on the first pull, though, if you prime it right. You gotta push that little red bulb exactly three times, no more, no less. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... over land or over water? There is that one overpass on the interstate, I guess, the one where they've been doing construction for the last six months. It's a nightmare trying to get through there during rush hour. Traffic just backs up for miles. I usually try to take the back roads, but then you get stuck behind the school buses. My daughter used to ride the bus, but she's driving now. Just got her license last month. She's a pretty good driver, actually, a lot better than my son was at her age. He backed right into the mailbox his first week having his license. Completely snapped the wooden post in half. I had to go to Home Depot and buy a whole new setup, get the quick-setting concrete, the whole nine yards. Digging that hole was a pain, too. Well, over land instead of water... I guess I'm just not sure. Like I said, it's the height that really gets me, so I don't know if the water makes a difference or not. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
A wide, flat area... I mean, I really couldn't tell you. High is high, right? Doesn't matter if it's water or flat dirt under me, I just don't like being up there. 

Speaking of flat, they actually just completely redid the main parking lot over at the distribution center. Totally leveled it out, repaved the whole thing. It was supposed to be this huge improvement, but the contractors they hired... man, they painted the lines all crooked. Now nobody knows where to park, and it's turning into a total HR nightmare because people are getting boxed in at the end of their shifts. Gary—that guy I was telling you about with the fake medical leave?—he drives this massive Ford F-250 and he just takes up two spots right near the entrance now. I told the facility manager we need to get the crew back out here to repaint, but he says it's not in the budget until next quarter. The budget's been super tight anyway since corporate switched our health insurance provider. The new copays are just ridiculous, and don't even get me started on the dental coverage. 

But wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the flat land under the bridge. Yeah, I don't think it would make a difference. If I'm up high, I'm going to be sweating it either way.

We actually went to the eye doctor last week, Dr. Henderson over in the strip mall next to the Target? Nice guy, but his waiting room is freezing. Anyway, he told her she needs bifocals now, which she wasn't too happy about. I'm almost always the one behind the wheel, so I couldn't really tell you if it's just as bad when I'm riding shotgun. I just know when I'm driving, it's... it's a whole ordeal.\n\nDoctor: If you were a passenger in a car going over a high bridge, would you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I'd still be pretty worked up even if I wasn't the one driving. I mean, just the sight of those bridges gets to me. As soon as I see the bridge coming up in the distance, my chest gets all tight, and my hands start sweating. Best thirty bucks I ever spent. It gets right into the crevices of the seats. \n\nBut, uh, wait... oh, right, being a passenger on the bridge. Yeah, I'm pretty sure my chest would still get tight and I'd be sweating just as bad, even if I wasn't the one steering. Just seeing the bridge is enough to set it off.\n\nDoctor: Has avoiding bridges or being late to work due to taking the long way around them affected your relationships with your coworkers or your overall job performance?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it hasn't really messed up my job or anything. I still get to work every day and do my job in HR, and I see people just fine. I just have to leave extra early to take the long way around the bridges. It's not a huge deal, but I do have to wake up at like 5:30 AM now. Which is fine, I guess, because I like to catch the morning news anyway. You know, they have that new anchor on Channel 4, the guy with the really great hair? He used to do the weather in Chicago, I think. My wife thinks he's too loud, but I like him. It's a shame, too, because they caught a ton of bass last time. My buddy Steve has this new fiberglass boat, a real beauty. He got it at a boat show in February. I think he paid too much for it, honestly, but he loves it. Anyway, yeah, I still get by, it's just... annoying having to plan around it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience similar anxiety in other situations where you feel like you're not in control?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think it's about being in control, because like I said, even if I'm not driving, just seeing the bridge gets me all worked up. It's really just the bridges. You know, they let you check two bags for free, which is great because my wife always overpacks. She brought like four pairs of shoes for a four-day trip. We went to this amazing seafood place right on the water down there. Best grouper sandwich I ever had. They served it with these homemade kettle chips that were just perfectly crispy. The waiter was a young kid, maybe 19, told us he was studying marine biology at the local college. Smart kid, very polite. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what were you asking? Oh, control. Yeah, no, I don't get anxious about that kind of stuff. It's literally just the high bridges. I don't know what it is about them, but nothing else really bothers me like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever walked or biked across a high bridge, and if so, did you experience anxiety in that situation?\n\nPatient: Walk or bike? Never won more than five bucks, though. My neighbor, Tom, he actually won like fifty grand on a scratch-off once. Can you believe that? He used it to put a sunroom on the back of his house. It was really nice, all glass, but it got so hot in the summer you couldn't even sit out there. He had to buy this massive ceiling fan just to keep the air moving. I helped him install it, actually. I just end up gripping it so hard my knuckles turn white, and I have to stare straight ahead, like I just have to make it to the other side as fast as possible. I don't even have to be on it yet. Just knowing I have to cross it and seeing it there... that's what gets me going.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety triggered by the height of the bridge or by the fact that you have to cross over water or a deep gap?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... I think it's the height, mostly. And the water. I know those bridges over the bay are built fine, you know? Like, folks drive them every day without a second thought. But when I get near one, my mind just acts like the car could go right over the side. It's crazy. Speaking of cars, I actually just had to take my Honda in for an oil change last week. The guy at the shop, Jerry, he's been working on my cars for years. He tried to tell me I needed new brake pads, but I just had them done like, I don't know, maybe two years ago? I told him to just do the oil. They always try to upsell you on something, you know? Like the air filters. I can change an air filter myself in five minutes, I'm not paying them forty bucks to do it. Jerry's a good guy, though. He coaches Little League with my brother-in-law. They had a really good season last year, made it all the way to the regionals. My nephew plays first base. Kid's got a swing like you wouldn't believe, but he strikes out a lot because he always swings for the fences. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the gap. Yeah, it's just... looking over the edge, seeing how far down it is to the water. It just makes me feel like we're gonna fall right off.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety triggered more by the fact that the bridge is over water, or would you feel just as anxious crossing a high bridge over land?\n\nPatient: Well, you know, I... I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about it like that. I don't think I cross a lot of high bridges over land. Most of the ones around here go over the water, like the big one heading out towards the shore. We used to go to the shore every summer when the kids were little. Rented a house down in Cape May. Beautiful Victorian houses down there. My wife loved the porches, always wanted to put a wrap-around porch on our house, but the zoning board wouldn't let us because of how close we are to the property line. The guy next door, Mr. Henderson, he's a real stickler for the rules. He actually called the town on us once because our grass was, like, half an inch over the limit. Can you believe that? Half an inch! I had to go out there on a Sunday morning and mow it just to get him to back off. My mower is getting pretty old, too. It's a Toro, had it for maybe twelve years. Still starts on the first pull, though, if you prime it right. You gotta push that little red bulb exactly three times, no more, no less. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... over land or over water? I guess I just don't really know for sure since I try to avoid them all anyway.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious crossing a very high bridge that goes over solid land?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, I... I don't really know if I'd feel any different if it was over land. I just don't run into those very often. There is that one overpass on the interstate, I guess, the one where they've been doing construction for the last six months. It's a nightmare trying to get through there during rush hour. Traffic just backs up for miles. I usually try to take the back roads, but then you get stuck behind the school buses. My daughter used to ride the bus, but she's driving now. Just got her license last month. She's a pretty good driver, actually, a lot better than my son was at her age. He backed right into the mailbox his first week having his license. Completely snapped the wooden post in half. I had to go to Home Depot and buy a whole new setup, get the quick-setting concrete, the whole nine yards. Digging that hole was a pain, too. Hit a huge rock about a foot down and had to dig around it. Took me all Saturday morning. \n\nBut, uh... a high bridge over land? I honestly couldn't tell you for sure. I just try to stay away from anything high up if I can help it.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel less anxious if you had to cross a very high bridge that goes over a deep valley with solid land at the bottom, rather than over water?\n\nPatient: Well, a deep valley... I really couldn't say if that'd be any better. I just don't like the height part, you know? Valleys actually remind me of when my brother-in-law went out to Colorado a couple years back. He went hiking somewhere near the Rockies, thought he was in great shape, but the altitude completely knocked him out. He was sick for three days straight in his hotel room. \n\nSpeaking of being out sick, work has been a total headache lately with that kind of stuff. I work in HR over at the distribution center, and we've got this one guy on the floor, Gary, who keeps trying to put in for medical leave every time he wants to take a ski trip. He thinks we don't check his social media, but I saw him posting pictures from Aspen when he was supposedly laid up with a bad back. I had to call him into the office on Tuesday and have a whole sit-down with him and the union rep. The union rep, Linda, she's always bringing these terrible store-bought muffins to our meetings. Like, the really dry ones that just crumble all over the desk and leave grease spots on the paperwork. \n\nBut, uh, wait, what was the question? Oh, right, a bridge over a valley. Yeah, I honestly have no idea if that would be less scary than the water. Like I said, I just hate being up high period, so I wouldn't really want to test it out either way.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were on a very high but enclosed bridge, like a tunnel bridge, over water?\n\nPatient: Well, an enclosed bridge... I don't know, maybe? I haven't really been on one of those. I mean, if I can't see the water, maybe it wouldn't be as bad. But then again, tunnels can be pretty awful too. We went through that tunnel up in Baltimore once, Fort McHenry, I think it's called? My wife was driving, and the traffic just stopped dead right in the middle. Turns out an eighteen-wheeler broke down in the right lane. \n\nThose truck drivers, they work crazy hours. We hire a lot of them at the distribution center, and they're always complaining about the electronic logging devices in the cabs. They say it tracks every little thing they do. One guy, Pete, he got so mad at it he actually tried to rip it out of the dashboard. Put a huge crack in the plastic. I had to write him up for destruction of company property. Pete's a good guy, though, just has a short fuse. He brought in this amazing barbecue brisket for the company potluck last summer. Smoked it for, like, fourteen hours, he said. \n\nBut, uh, an enclosed bridge... I'm really not sure. I guess if I knew how high up I was, I'd still be pretty nervous. I just try to avoid the whole situation, you know?\n\nDoctor: Would you feel less anxious if you were on a bridge of the same height, but over land with no water underneath?\n\nPatient: Well, over land instead of water... I guess I'm just not sure. Like I said, it's the height that really gets me, so I don't know if the water makes a difference or not. Speaking of land, my neighbor just bought this massive plot of land out in the country. He's talking about building a cabin out there, entirely off the grid. Solar panels, well water, the whole bit. \n\nHe asked if I wanted to go out there this weekend to help him clear some brush, but my back has been killing me lately. I think I slept wrong on it, or maybe it's this new mattress my wife insisted we buy. It's one of those memory foam ones that comes rolled up in a box. You cut the plastic and it just inflates like a life raft. Honestly, I hate it. It's like sleeping in wet sand, you just sink right in and can't turn over. I tried to tell her we should have just gone to a regular mattress store, but she had this coupon code from a podcast she listens to. It's one of those true crime ones. She listens to them while she cooks, which is fine, I guess, but hearing about unsolved murders while I'm trying to eat my meatloaf is a little unsettling. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, the bridge over land. Yeah, I really don't think it would matter much. High up is high up, you know? I just don't like it.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel less anxious crossing a bridge of the same height if it were over a wide, flat area of land rather than over water?\n\nPatient: A wide, flat area... I mean, I really couldn't tell you. High is high, right? Doesn't matter if it's water or flat dirt under me, I just don't like being up there. \n\nSpeaking of flat, they actually just completely redid the main parking lot over at the distribution center. Totally leveled it out, repaved the whole thing. It was supposed to be this huge improvement, but the contractors they hired... man, they painted the lines all crooked. Now nobody knows where to park, and it's turning into a total HR nightmare because people are getting boxed in at the end of their shifts. Gary—that guy I was telling you about with the fake medical leave?—he drives this massive Ford F-250 and he just takes up two spots right near the entrance now. I told the facility manager we need to get the crew back out here to repaint, but he says it's not in the budget until next quarter. The budget's been super tight anyway since corporate switched our health insurance provider. The new copays are just ridiculous, and don't even get me started on the dental coverage. \n\nBut wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the flat land under the bridge. Yeah, I don't think it would make a difference. If I'm up high, I'm going to be sweating it either way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, a dry riverbed... I really don't think so. I've been trying to say, it's just the height that bothers me. Water, dirt, dry rocks... it doesn't really change the fact that I'm way up in the air. 

Speaking of dry, though, we haven't had a decent rain in weeks. My lawn is completely burnt to a crisp. I tried setting up the sprinkler on a timer, but the hose got a kink in it and I didn't notice for three days. My wife was pretty annoyed because she just planted these expensive hydrangeas she got from the nursery down on Route 9. The lady there told her they needed a ton of water, so of course, they're looking pretty sad right now. I told her we should just put down artificial turf in the backyard and be done with it. A guy at work did that—Dave from payroll. He says it looks perfect year-round and he never has to mow it. Plus, his dog can't dig holes in it. Dave's got this huge golden retriever that used to destroy his flowerbeds. Cute dog, though. He brings him into the office sometimes on Fridays. 

But, uh, what was the question? The dry riverbed? Yeah, no, I don't think it matters what's under the bridge. High is high, and I just try to stay away from it.

An empty parking lot... Doc, I feel like we're going in circles here. Like I've been saying, it really doesn't matter what's down there. Empty parking lot, dry dirt, water... if it's a high bridge, my stomach is going to be in knots either way. 

Speaking of empty parking lots, though, you should see the old strip mall off I-95. The one where the Circuit City used to be? Totally abandoned now. Nothing but weeds coming up through the cracks. The other night, I was driving past there on my way back from picking up dinner—my wife wanted Chinese, but the place we usually go to closed down, so I had to drive all the way across town to this new spot. Anyway, I drive past that empty lot and there are these teenagers doing donuts in some beat-up Honda Civic. Just tearing the place up, tire smoke everywhere. 

It made me think of my nephew, actually. He just got his learner's permit and he's already begging my sister to co-sign for a sports car. I told her she's crazy if she does it. Do you know what the auto insurance premiums look like for a sixteen-year-old boy in a Mustang? I see the payroll deductions come across my desk at work sometimes for guys putting their teenage sons on their plans, and it's practically a second mortgage. I keep telling my sister she needs to just get him a used Volvo or something built like a tank. 

But, uh... sorry, I lost my train of thought again. Right, the bridge over the parking lot. Yeah, no, it wouldn't make me feel any better. The height is the whole problem, you know? Just being up that high is what gets to me.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
High walls? Hmm... I guess if I couldn't see over the edge, maybe? I don't know, honestly. I've never really been on a bridge like that. If I can't see the drop, maybe my brain wouldn't freak out as much. 

Speaking of high walls, though, you wouldn't believe the privacy fence my neighbor just put up. It's gotta be, like, eight feet tall. Solid wood, right along the property line. He didn't even talk to me about it first, just hired this crew that showed up at 7 AM on a Saturday with post-hole diggers. Woke up the whole house. My wife was furious because she likes to sleep in on weekends after working the late shift at the diner. And the worst part is, the fence completely blocks the morning sun from hitting my tomato plants. I've been trying to grow these heirloom beefsteaks all summer, and now they're just sitting there in the shade, staying green. 

I tried talking to him about it—Bob's his name, works over at the water treatment plant—but he just mumbled something about his wife wanting more privacy for their hot tub. A hot tub! Can you imagine the electric bill on one of those things? I see the utility deductions for some of the guys at work who have pools and spas, and it's insane. Plus, the maintenance... you gotta buy all those chemicals. 

Anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, right, the bridge with the walls. Yeah, I mean, maybe it would help a little if I couldn't see down, but I'm really not sure. I just try to avoid bridges altogether if I can help it.

I don't know how he deals with a boat, honestly, the maintenance alone is a total nightmare. \n\nBut, uh, anyway, yeah. The bridges. If I absolutely can't find a route around them, I just white-knuckle the steering wheel the whole way across. Just gripping it for dear life. We had this one guy last week showing up at 10 AM every single day, just strolling in like he owned the place, and I had to be the one to sit him down and talk about company time... but yeah, the driving thing is just getting to be a real hassle.\n\nDoctor: Do you also avoid other high places, like tall buildings or mountainous areas, or is it mainly just the bridges that bother you?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the bridges. Tall buildings don't bother me at all. Actually, our old HR office used to be on the 14th floor of this big high-rise downtown before we moved out to the business park. The elevators in that place, though... man, they were always breaking down. I swear, at least once a month somebody would get stuck in one. Actually, he just got new tires on that thing, those big all-terrain ones. He got a flat last month on his way to work and had to wait three hours for a tow truck. Three hours! I told him he should have just put the spare on himself, but he said the jack was rusted out. You really gotta check your tools before winter hits, you know? The salt on the roads around here just eats right through the metal. I usually take my car to the wash at least twice a month during the winter just to spray the undercarriage. They have this new automated one down by the grocery store that does a really good job with the wax, too, and it's only like eight bucks if you buy the monthly pass. \n\nBut, uh... sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the bridge falling down. Yeah, I'm not worried about the engineering or anything like that. It's just... when I get near one, my mind just acts like the car could go right over the side. Like I'm just going to veer off the edge or something.\n\nDoctor: Does your anxiety occur when you are a passenger in a car driving over a high bridge, or is it only when you are the one driving?\n\nPatient: Well, it's funny you ask that, because I haven't actually been a passenger over one in a long time. My wife usually makes me drive everywhere. She hates driving, especially at night. Her night vision isn't what it used to be. We actually went to the eye doctor last week, Dr. Henderson over in the strip mall next to the Target? Nice guy, but his waiting room is freezing. I don't know why doctors' offices always keep the AC cranked up so high. You sit there for twenty minutes and you're shivering by the time they call your name. Anyway, he told her she needs bifocals now, which she wasn't too happy about. She thinks they make her look old, but I told her, hey, they have those progressive lenses now where you can't even see the line. My buddy Carl has them and he swears by them. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... what was the question? Oh, being a passenger. Yeah, I don't know, honestly. I'm almost always the one behind the wheel, so I couldn't really tell you if it's just as bad when I'm riding shotgun. I just know when I'm driving, it's... it's a whole ordeal.\n\nDoctor: If you were a passenger in a car going over a high bridge, would you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I'd still be pretty worked up even if I wasn't the one driving. I mean, just the sight of those bridges gets to me. As soon as I see the bridge coming up in the distance, my chest gets all tight, and my hands start sweating. Even if I was in the passenger seat, I think I'd still be gripping the door handle or something, just staring straight ahead, feeling like we just have to make it to the other side, you know? \n\nIt's funny, speaking of the passenger seat, my wife has this habit of leaving her empty coffee cups in the door pocket on her side. I swear, every time I go to vacuum out the car—which I try to do every other weekend, especially with the dog tracking in dirt—I find like three of those paper cups from the Dunkin' down the street. I keep telling her, \"Honey, just throw them out when we get gas,\" but she always forgets. We've got this great little hand vac, though, a Dirt Devil. Got it on sale at Home Depot last Black Friday. Best thirty bucks I ever spent. It gets right into the crevices of the seats. \n\nBut, uh, wait... oh, right, being a passenger on the bridge. Yeah, I'm pretty sure my chest would still get tight and I'd be sweating just as bad, even if I wasn't the one steering. Just seeing the bridge is enough to set it off.\n\nDoctor: Has avoiding bridges or being late to work due to taking the long way around them affected your relationships with your coworkers or your overall job performance?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it hasn't really messed up my job or anything. I still get to work every day and do my job in HR, and I see people just fine. I just have to leave extra early to take the long way around the bridges. It's not a huge deal, but I do have to wake up at like 5:30 AM now. Which is fine, I guess, because I like to catch the morning news anyway. You know, they have that new anchor on Channel 4, the guy with the really great hair? He used to do the weather in Chicago, I think. My wife thinks he's too loud, but I like him. He actually did a whole segment on how to properly winterize your lawn sprinkler system last week. I meant to write down the steps, because last year one of my pipes burst and it was a nightmare. Water everywhere, right in the front yard. Had to call a plumber on a Sunday, which you know costs an arm and a leg. He charged me double just for showing up. \n\nBut, uh, what was I saying? Oh, right, work and stuff. Yeah, my job's fine. It's more just... I don't know, it makes me feel less independent than I used to be. Like, I've had to turn down a few fishing outings with the guys because the lake is over the big suspension bridge. I just told them I was busy, but really I just didn't want to deal with the drive. It's a shame, too, because they caught a ton of bass last time. My buddy Steve has this new fiberglass boat, a real beauty. He got it at a boat show in February. I think he paid too much for it, honestly, but he loves it. Anyway, yeah, I still get by, it's just... annoying having to plan around it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience similar anxiety in other situations where you feel like you're not in control?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think it's about being in control, because like I said, even if I'm not driving, just seeing the bridge gets me all worked up. It's really just the bridges. I fly on planes just fine, you know? Actually, my wife and I flew down to Florida last winter to visit her sister in Tampa. We flew Southwest. You know, they let you check two bags for free, which is great because my wife always overpacks. She brought like four pairs of shoes for a four-day trip. We went to this amazing seafood place right on the water down there. Best grouper sandwich I ever had. They served it with these homemade kettle chips that were just perfectly crispy. The waiter was a young kid, maybe 19, told us he was studying marine biology at the local college. Smart kid, very polite. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what were you asking? Oh, control. Yeah, no, I don't get anxious about that kind of stuff. It's literally just the high bridges. I don't know what it is about them, but nothing else really bothers me like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever walked or biked across a high bridge, and if so, did you experience anxiety in that situation?\n\nPatient: Walk or bike? Oh, no, I haven't tried that. I haven't ridden a bike in... gosh, it's gotta be fifteen years. I used to have this great mountain bike, a Schwinn, back when we lived in our old neighborhood. It had twenty-one gears and these really thick tires. I used to ride it down to the corner store on Saturday mornings to get the paper and a couple of scratch-offs. Never won more than five bucks, though. My neighbor, Tom, he actually won like fifty grand on a scratch-off once. Can you believe that? He used it to put a sunroom on the back of his house. It was really nice, all glass, but it got so hot in the summer you couldn't even sit out there. He had to buy this massive ceiling fan just to keep the air moving. I helped him install it, actually. We almost dropped the motor right on his glass coffee table. \n\nBut, uh, what was the question? Oh, walking or biking over a bridge. Yeah, no, I haven't done that. I can't even imagine it. If just driving over one or seeing it from the passenger seat gets me sweating and my chest all tight, I think walking across one would probably be a hundred times worse. Just being out there in the open air, looking down... no thanks. I wouldn't even try it.\n\nDoctor: Is it the sight of the bridge itself that triggers your anxiety, or is it something else about the experience of being on the bridge?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's definitely just the sight of it. Like, as soon as I see the bridge coming up in the distance, my chest gets all tight, and my hands start sweating on the steering wheel. I just end up gripping it so hard my knuckles turn white, and I have to stare straight ahead, like I just have to make it to the other side as fast as possible. It's the same feeling I got when I had to give that presentation at work last year. We were rolling out this new benefits package, and my boss made me stand up in front of the whole department. I hate public speaking. My mouth gets dry as a bone. I actually kept a bottle of water up there with me, one of those fancy Fiji waters in the square bottle. My wife buys them by the case at Costco. You know, Costco is great, but you end up buying way more than you need. We went in for paper towels once and came out with a gazebo. A whole gazebo! Took me three weekends to put it together in the backyard. The instructions were terrible, just pictures, no words. \n\nBut, uh... where was I? Oh, the bridges. Yeah, it's just seeing them. I don't even have to be on it yet. Just knowing I have to cross it and seeing it there... that's what gets me going.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety triggered by the height of the bridge or by the fact that you have to cross over water or a deep gap?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... I think it's the height, mostly. And the water. I know those bridges over the bay are built fine, you know? Like, folks drive them every day without a second thought. But when I get near one, my mind just acts like the car could go right over the side. It's crazy. Speaking of cars, I actually just had to take my Honda in for an oil change last week. The guy at the shop, Jerry, he's been working on my cars for years. He tried to tell me I needed new brake pads, but I just had them done like, I don't know, maybe two years ago? I told him to just do the oil. They always try to upsell you on something, you know? Like the air filters. I can change an air filter myself in five minutes, I'm not paying them forty bucks to do it. Jerry's a good guy, though. He coaches Little League with my brother-in-law. They had a really good season last year, made it all the way to the regionals. My nephew plays first base. Kid's got a swing like you wouldn't believe, but he strikes out a lot because he always swings for the fences. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the gap. Yeah, it's just... looking over the edge, seeing how far down it is to the water. It just makes me feel like we're gonna fall right off.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety triggered more by the fact that the bridge is over water, or would you feel just as anxious crossing a high bridge over land?\n\nPatient: Well, you know, I... I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about it like that. I don't think I cross a lot of high bridges over land. Most of the ones around here go over the water, like the big one heading out towards the shore. We used to go to the shore every summer when the kids were little. Rented a house down in Cape May. Beautiful Victorian houses down there. My wife loved the porches, always wanted to put a wrap-around porch on our house, but the zoning board wouldn't let us because of how close we are to the property line. The guy next door, Mr. Henderson, he's a real stickler for the rules. He actually called the town on us once because our grass was, like, half an inch over the limit. Can you believe that? Half an inch! I had to go out there on a Sunday morning and mow it just to get him to back off. My mower is getting pretty old, too. It's a Toro, had it for maybe twelve years. Still starts on the first pull, though, if you prime it right. You gotta push that little red bulb exactly three times, no more, no less. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... over land or over water? I guess I just don't really know for sure since I try to avoid them all anyway.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious crossing a very high bridge that goes over solid land?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, I... I don't really know if I'd feel any different if it was over land. I just don't run into those very often. There is that one overpass on the interstate, I guess, the one where they've been doing construction for the last six months. It's a nightmare trying to get through there during rush hour. Traffic just backs up for miles. I usually try to take the back roads, but then you get stuck behind the school buses. My daughter used to ride the bus, but she's driving now. Just got her license last month. She's a pretty good driver, actually, a lot better than my son was at her age. He backed right into the mailbox his first week having his license. Completely snapped the wooden post in half. I had to go to Home Depot and buy a whole new setup, get the quick-setting concrete, the whole nine yards. Digging that hole was a pain, too. Hit a huge rock about a foot down and had to dig around it. Took me all Saturday morning. \n\nBut, uh... a high bridge over land? I honestly couldn't tell you for sure. I just try to stay away from anything high up if I can help it.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel less anxious if you had to cross a very high bridge that goes over a deep valley with solid land at the bottom, rather than over water?\n\nPatient: Well, a deep valley... I really couldn't say if that'd be any better. I just don't like the height part, you know? Valleys actually remind me of when my brother-in-law went out to Colorado a couple years back. He went hiking somewhere near the Rockies, thought he was in great shape, but the altitude completely knocked him out. He was sick for three days straight in his hotel room. \n\nSpeaking of being out sick, work has been a total headache lately with that kind of stuff. I work in HR over at the distribution center, and we've got this one guy on the floor, Gary, who keeps trying to put in for medical leave every time he wants to take a ski trip. He thinks we don't check his social media, but I saw him posting pictures from Aspen when he was supposedly laid up with a bad back. I had to call him into the office on Tuesday and have a whole sit-down with him and the union rep. The union rep, Linda, she's always bringing these terrible store-bought muffins to our meetings. Like, the really dry ones that just crumble all over the desk and leave grease spots on the paperwork. \n\nBut, uh, wait, what was the question? Oh, right, a bridge over a valley. Yeah, I honestly have no idea if that would be less scary than the water. Like I said, I just hate being up high period, so I wouldn't really want to test it out either way.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were on a very high but enclosed bridge, like a tunnel bridge, over water?\n\nPatient: Well, an enclosed bridge... I don't know, maybe? I haven't really been on one of those. I mean, if I can't see the water, maybe it wouldn't be as bad. But then again, tunnels can be pretty awful too. We went through that tunnel up in Baltimore once, Fort McHenry, I think it's called? My wife was driving, and the traffic just stopped dead right in the middle. Turns out an eighteen-wheeler broke down in the right lane. \n\nThose truck drivers, they work crazy hours. We hire a lot of them at the distribution center, and they're always complaining about the electronic logging devices in the cabs. They say it tracks every little thing they do. I guess I'm just not sure. Like I said, it's the height that really gets me, so I don't know if the water makes a difference or not.
