I just don't like it.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel less anxious crossing a bridge of the same height if it were over a wide, flat area of land rather than over water?\n\nPatient: A wide, flat area... Hit a huge rock about a foot down and had to dig around it. Took me all Saturday morning. \n\nBut, uh... a high bridge over land? I honestly couldn't tell you for sure. I just try to stay away from anything high up if I can help it.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel less anxious if you had to cross a very high bridge that goes over a deep valley with solid land at the bottom, rather than over water?\n\nPatient: Well, a deep valley... I really couldn't say if that'd be any better. I just don't like the height part, you know? Valleys actually remind me of when my brother-in-law went out to Colorado a couple years back. He went hiking somewhere near the Rockies, thought he was in great shape, but the altitude completely knocked him out. He was sick for three days straight in his hotel room. \n\nSpeaking of being out sick, work has been a total headache lately with that kind of stuff. I work in HR over at the distribution center, and we've got this one guy on the floor, Gary, who keeps trying to put in for medical leave every time he wants to take a ski trip. He thinks we don't check his social media, but I saw him posting pictures from Aspen when he was supposedly laid up with a bad back. I had to call him into the office on Tuesday and have a whole sit-down with him and the union rep. The union rep, Linda, she's always bringing these terrible store-bought muffins to our meetings. Like, the really dry ones that just crumble all over the desk and leave grease spots on the paperwork. \n\nBut, uh, wait, what was the question? Oh, right, a bridge over a valley. Yeah, I honestly have no idea if that would be less scary than the water. Like I said, I just hate being up high period, so I wouldn't really want to test it out either way.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were on a very high but enclosed bridge, like a tunnel bridge, over water?\n\nPatient: Well, an enclosed bridge... I don't know, maybe? I haven't really been on one of those. I mean, if I can't see the water, maybe it wouldn't be as bad. But then again, tunnels can be pretty awful too. We went through that tunnel up in Baltimore once, Fort McHenry, I think it's called? My wife was driving, and the traffic just stopped dead right in the middle. Turns out an eighteen-wheeler broke down in the right lane. \n\nThose truck drivers, they work crazy hours. We hire a lot of them at the distribution center, and they're always complaining about the electronic logging devices in the cabs. They say it tracks every little thing they do. One guy, Pete, he got so mad at it he actually tried to rip it out of the dashboard. Put a huge crack in the plastic. I had to write him up for destruction of company property. Pete's a good guy, though, just has a short fuse. He brought in this amazing barbecue brisket for the company potluck last summer. Smoked it for, like, fourteen hours, he said. \n\nBut, uh, an enclosed bridge... I'm really not sure. I guess if I knew how high up I was, I'd still be pretty nervous. I just try to avoid the whole situation, you know?\n\nDoctor: Would you feel less anxious if you were on a bridge of the same height, but over land with no water underneath?\n\nPatient: Well, over land instead of water... I guess I'm just not sure. Like I said, it's the height that really gets me, so I don't know if the water makes a difference or not. Speaking of land, my neighbor just bought this massive plot of land out in the country. He's talking about building a cabin out there, entirely off the grid. Solar panels, well water, the whole bit. \n\nHe asked if I wanted to go out there this weekend to help him clear some brush, but my back has been killing me lately. I think I slept wrong on it, or maybe it's this new mattress my wife insisted we buy. It's one of those memory foam ones that comes rolled up in a box. You cut the plastic and it just inflates like a life raft. Honestly, I hate it. It's like sleeping in wet sand, you just sink right in and can't turn over. I tried to tell her we should have just gone to a regular mattress store, but she had this coupon code from a podcast she listens to. It's one of those true crime ones. She listens to them while she cooks, which is fine, I guess, but hearing about unsolved murders while I'm trying to eat my meatloaf is a little unsettling. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, the bridge over land. Yeah, I really don't think it would matter much. High up is high up, you know? I just don't like it.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel less anxious crossing a bridge of the same height if it were over a wide, flat area of land rather than over water?\n\nPatient: A wide, flat area... I mean, I really couldn't tell you. High is high, right? Doesn't matter if it's water or flat dirt under me, I just don't like being up there. \n\nSpeaking of flat, they actually just completely redid the main parking lot over at the distribution center. Totally leveled it out, repaved the whole thing. It was supposed to be this huge improvement, but the contractors they hired... man, they painted the lines all crooked. Now nobody knows where to park, and it's turning into a total HR nightmare because people are getting boxed in at the end of their shifts. Gary—that guy I was telling you about with the fake medical leave?—he drives this massive Ford F-250 and he just takes up two spots right near the entrance now. I told the facility manager we need to get the crew back out here to repaint, but he says it's not in the budget until next quarter. The budget's been super tight anyway since corporate switched our health insurance provider. The new copays are just ridiculous, and don't even get me started on the dental coverage. \n\nBut wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the flat land under the bridge. Yeah, I don't think it would make a difference. If I'm up high, I'm going to be sweating it either way.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel less anxious crossing a very high bridge over a dry riverbed or a wide, flat area of land?\n\nPatient: Well, a dry riverbed... I really don't think so. I've been trying to say, it's just the height that bothers me. Water, dirt, dry rocks... it doesn't really change the fact that I'm way up in the air. \n\nSpeaking of dry, though, we haven't had a decent rain in weeks. My lawn is completely burnt to a crisp. I tried setting up the sprinkler on a timer, but the hose got a kink in it and I didn't notice for three days. My wife was pretty annoyed because she just planted these expensive hydrangeas she got from the nursery down on Route 9. The lady there told her they needed a ton of water, so of course, they're looking pretty sad right now. I told her we should just put down artificial turf in the backyard and be done with it. A guy at work did that—Dave from payroll. He says it looks perfect year-round and he never has to mow it. Plus, his dog can't dig holes in it. Dave's got this huge golden retriever that used to destroy his flowerbeds. Cute dog, though. He brings him into the office sometimes on Fridays. \n\nBut, uh, what was the question? The dry riverbed? Yeah, no, I don't think it matters what's under the bridge. High is high, and I just try to stay away from it.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel less anxious if you had to cross a bridge of the same height over a large, empty parking lot?\n\nPatient: An empty parking lot... Doc, I feel like we're going in circles here. Like I've been saying, it really doesn't matter what's down there. Empty parking lot, dry dirt, water... if it's a high bridge, my stomach is going to be in knots either way. \n\nSpeaking of empty parking lots, though, you should see the old strip mall off I-95. The one where the Circuit City used to be? Totally abandoned now. Nothing but weeds coming up through the cracks. The other night, I was driving past there on my way back from picking up dinner—my wife wanted Chinese, but the place we usually go to closed down, so I had to drive all the way across town to this new spot. Anyway, I drive past that empty lot and there are these teenagers doing donuts in some beat-up Honda Civic. Just tearing the place up, tire smoke everywhere. \n\nIt made me think of my nephew, actually. He just got his learner's permit and he's already begging my sister to co-sign for a sports car. I told her she's crazy if she does it. Do you know what the auto insurance premiums look like for a sixteen-year-old boy in a Mustang? I see the payroll deductions come across my desk at work sometimes for guys putting their teenage sons on their plans, and it's practically a second mortgage. I keep telling my sister she needs to just get him a used Volvo or something built like a tank. \n\nBut, uh... sorry, I lost my train of thought again. Right, the bridge over the parking lot. Yeah, no, it wouldn't make me feel any better. The height is the whole problem, you know? Just being up that high is what gets to me.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety decrease if you were crossing a bridge of the same height, but in a situation where you couldn't see the height, such as a bridge with very high walls on both sides?\n\nPatient: High walls? Hmm... I guess if I couldn't see over the edge, maybe? I don't know, honestly. I've never really been on a bridge like that. If I can't see the drop, maybe my brain wouldn't freak out as much. \n\nSpeaking of high walls, though, you wouldn't believe the privacy fence my neighbor just put up. It's gotta be, like, eight feet tall. Solid wood, right along the property line. He didn't even talk to me about it first, just hired this crew that showed up at 7 AM on a Saturday with post-hole diggers. Woke up the whole house. My wife was furious because she likes to sleep in on weekends after working the late shift at the diner. And the worst part is, the fence completely blocks the morning sun from hitting my tomato plants. I've been trying to grow these heirloom beefsteaks all summer, and now they're just sitting there in the shade, staying green. \n\nI tried talking to him about it—Bob's his name, works over at the water treatment plant—but he just mumbled something about his wife wanting more privacy for their hot tub. A hot tub! Can you imagine the electric bill on one of those things? I see the utility deductions for some of the guys at work who have pools and spas, and it's insane. Plus, the maintenance... you gotta buy all those chemicals. \n\nAnyway... what were we talking about? Oh, right, the bridge with the walls. Yeah, I mean, maybe it would help a little if I couldn't see down, but I'm really not sure. I just try to avoid bridges altogether if I can help it."
  }
